Register Shifts Break LLM Safety: A Bengali Benchmark with Culturally Grounded Harms
Authors: Naymul Islam, Nusrat Jahan Lia, Shubhashis Roy Dipta, Sabik Bin Sultan, Abdullah Khan Zehady
First: 2026-08-23T10:09:00+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-23T10:09:00+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Bengali is the seventh-most-spoken language globally, yet LLM safety evaluation remains overwhelmingly English-centric. We introduce BanglaSafe, a benchmark of 879 Bengali prompts combining 309 natively authored prompts with 570 expert-reviewed prompts, spanning 17 culturally grounded harm categories and five prompting conditions that vary language, writing style, and authority framing. Evaluating 18 frontier LLMs, we find that over half of all responses are unsafe or partially unsafe (53.6%) while 14.7% contains strictly harmful content, and that the strongest observed effect is not the switch from English to Bengali but the choice of writing style within Bengali: the same harmful request phrased as a formal newspaper investigation succeeds 17 percentage points more often than the same request phrased as a casual message, with no adversarial engineering involved. We further show that existing safety classifiers struggle to reliably evaluate Bengali content, with even frontier models failing on nearly half of all cases.
中文标题/摘要
标题：注册班次突破LLM安全：一个具有文化基础危害的孟加拉基准
孟加拉语是全球第七大语言，但LLM安全评估仍然以英语为中心。我们推出了BanglaSafe，这是一个包含879个孟加拉语提示的基准，结合了309个本土创作的提示和570个专家审查的提示，涵盖17个文化基础的危害类别和五种变化语言、写作风格和权威框架的提示条件。评估18个前沿LLM，我们发现超过一半的响应是不安全或部分不安全的（53.6%），而14.7%的内容包含严格有害的信息，观察到的最强效应不是从英语切换到孟加拉语，而是在孟加拉语中选择写作风格：同样的有害请求以正式的报纸调查形式表达时成功率比以随意消息形式表达时高出17个百分点，且没有涉及对抗性工程。我们进一步表明，现有的安全分类器在可靠评估孟加拉语内容方面存在困难，甚至前沿模型在近一半的案例中也未能成功。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the lack of safety evaluation for large language models (LLMs) in Bengali, the seventh-most-spoken language, which has been predominantly focused on English. Previous methods have been inadequate in assessing culturally specific harms, leading to a gap in understanding LLM safety in non-English contexts. The proposed approach, BanglaSafe, introduces a benchmark of 879 Bengali prompts that encompass a variety of culturally grounded harm categories and prompting conditions, effectively addressing the limitations of existing safety evaluations. The methodology involves evaluating 18 leading LLMs against this benchmark, revealing that over half of the responses are unsafe, with significant variations in safety based on writing style. This study contributes to the field by highlighting the inadequacies of current safety classifiers for Bengali content and demonstrating the need for culturally relevant evaluation metrics.
本研究关注于大型语言模型（LLMs）在孟加拉语中的安全性评估缺失，而孟加拉语是全球第七大语言，安全性评估主要集中在英语上。以往的方法主要在英语中评估LLMs，导致对非英语语言，特别是孟加拉语的评估不足，文化细微差别被忽视。提出的方法BanglaSafe引入了一个包含879个孟加拉语提示的基准，其中包括本土创作和专家审核的提示，涵盖各种危害类别和提示条件，有效解决了现有方法的不足。本文的贡献在于揭示了对18个评估的LLMs的响应中，超过一半是不安全或部分不安全的，并且孟加拉语提示中使用的写作风格对安全性有显著影响。研究结果表明，现有的安全分类器对孟加拉语内容的评估效果不佳，强调了在多语言环境中需要量身定制的评估方法。
Redteaming Leading Arabic LLMs with ASAS
Authors: Fidaa Abed, Haidar Khan, M Saiful Bari, Babar Khan, Abdalghani Abujabal
First: 2026-08-22T14:34:08+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-22T14:34:08+00:00
Comments: 13 pages, 5 figues, 3 tables
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
As the adoption of large language models (LLMs) grows in Arabic-speaking regions, ensuring their safety and cultural alignment is increasingly critical. However, Arabic LLM safety remains underexplored, especially in adversarial evaluation settings. We introduce the Arabic Safety Index (ASAS), the first fully human-curated Arabic benchmark for redteaming LLMs. ASAS contains 801 prompts spanning 8 safety categories and 8 attack strategies, with ideal responses in Modern Standard Arabic (MSA). We conduct a redteaming evaluation across seven leading models with Arabic capabilities, including GPT-4o, Claude 3.7 Sonnet, and regional models such as ALLaM and FANAR. Human annotators rate responses using a structured 4-point safety scale, revealing that most models fail to defend against 50% of unsafe prompts. Our findings highlight major safety gaps in high-harm categories such as weapons and illicit substances, with direct and obfuscation-based attacks proving most effective. The results also show that language alignment does not readily transfer across languages, and that automated safety judges (e.g., GPT-4o) perform poorly compared to human annotators. ASAS provides a culturally grounded benchmark and redteaming protocol to drive progress in Arabic LLM safety.
中文标题/摘要
标题：使用ASAS对领先的阿拉伯语LLM进行红队测试
随着大型语言模型（LLMs）在阿拉伯语地区的应用不断增长，确保其安全性和文化一致性变得愈发重要。然而，阿拉伯语LLM的安全性仍然未得到充分探索，尤其是在对抗性评估环境中。我们引入了阿拉伯安全指数（ASAS），这是第一个完全由人工策划的阿拉伯基准，用于对LLMs进行红队测试。ASAS包含801个提示，涵盖8个安全类别和8种攻击策略，并提供现代标准阿拉伯语（MSA）的理想响应。我们对七个具有阿拉伯语能力的领先模型进行了红队评估，包括GPT-4o、Claude 3.7 Sonnet以及区域模型如ALLaM和FANAR。人类注释者使用结构化的4分安全评分标准对响应进行评分，结果显示大多数模型未能抵御50%的不安全提示。我们的研究结果突显了在武器和非法物质等高危类别中的主要安全缺口，直接攻击和混淆攻击被证明最为有效。结果还表明，语言一致性并不能轻易跨语言转移，自动安全评估者（例如GPT-4o）的表现远不及人类注释者。ASAS提供了一个文化基础的基准和红队测试协议，以推动阿拉伯语LLM安全性的进展。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical need for safety and cultural alignment in Arabic large language models (LLMs), an area that has been largely overlooked, particularly in adversarial evaluations. Previous methods lacked comprehensive benchmarks for assessing safety in Arabic LLMs, leading to significant gaps in understanding their vulnerabilities. The proposed Arabic Safety Index (ASAS) introduces a human-curated benchmark specifically designed for redteaming LLMs, featuring 801 prompts across various safety categories and attack strategies. This methodology allows for a structured evaluation of leading Arabic models, revealing that many fail to adequately respond to unsafe prompts, particularly in high-harm areas. The findings demonstrate that automated safety assessments are less effective than human evaluations, underscoring the necessity of ASAS in enhancing the safety of Arabic LLMs and providing a foundation for future research in this domain.
本研究解决了阿拉伯语地区使用的大型语言模型（LLMs）在安全性和文化对齐方面的关键需求，这一领域在对抗性评估中被严重忽视。以往的方法缺乏全面的基准来评估阿拉伯LLM的安全性，导致对其脆弱性的理解存在重大缺口。提出的阿拉伯安全指数（ASAS）引入了一个人类策划的基准，包含801个跨多个安全类别和攻击策略的提示，从而能够更全面地评估LLM。这一方法涉及对七个具有阿拉伯语能力的领先模型进行红队评估，结果显示大多数模型未能有效应对50%的不安全提示，尤其是在高危害类别中。研究结果强调了改善阿拉伯LLM安全性措施的必要性，并证明ASAS可以有效指导该领域未来的研究。
CLEAR: Continuous Latent Adapter Routing for Utility-Preserving LLM Safety Alignment
Authors: Chengxiao Wang, Enyi Jiang, Xiaojing Liao, Sanmi Koyejo
First: 2026-08-21T16:36:10+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-21T16:36:10+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Improving the safety of large language models (LLMs) often comes at the expense of utility, as globally applied safety tuning may affect model responses to both harmful and benign inputs. We propose \textbf{C}ontinuous \textbf{L}at\textbf{E}nt \textbf{A}dapter \textbf{R}outing (CLEAR), a conditional safety adaptation framework that uses a lightweight hidden-state gate to continuously control the activation strength of a safety low-rank adapter. CLEAR aims to reduce harmful completions while avoiding unnecessary changes to the frozen backbone that could degrade performance on benign prompts. Experiments on widely used safety and utility benchmarks show that CLEAR improves robustness on HarmBench while reducing the utility degradation observed with globally applied safety tuning such as SFT or standard low-rank adaptation (LoRA). On Llama-3-8B-Instruct, CLEAR reduces HarmBench ASR from 32.3\% to 0.5\%, while retaining most of the base model's utility and achieving up to 7.1 percentage points higher GSM8K accuracy than globally applied SFT or LoRA. These results suggest that CLEAR is a promising mechanism for improving the safety--utility trade-off in LLM alignment.
中文标题/摘要
标题：CLEAR：用于保持效用的LLM安全对齐的连续潜在适配器路由
提高大型语言模型（LLM）的安全性往往会以效用为代价，因为全局应用的安全调优可能会影响模型对有害和良性输入的响应。我们提出了\textbf{C}ontinuous \textbf{L}at\textbf{E}nt \textbf{A}dapter \textbf{R}outing（CLEAR），这是一种条件安全适应框架，使用轻量级的隐藏状态门控来持续控制安全低秩适配器的激活强度。CLEAR旨在减少有害的完成，同时避免对冻结主干的不必要更改，这可能会降低对良性提示的性能。对广泛使用的安全和效用基准的实验表明，CLEAR在HarmBench上提高了鲁棒性，同时减少了全局应用安全调优（如SFT或标准低秩适配（LoRA））所观察到的效用下降。在Llama-3-8B-Instruct上，CLEAR将HarmBench ASR从32.3\%降低到0.5\%，同时保留了大部分基础模型的效用，并在GSM8K准确率上比全局应用的SFT或LoRA高出多达7.1个百分点。这些结果表明，CLEAR是改善LLM对齐中安全与效用权衡的有希望的机制。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of enhancing the safety of large language models (LLMs) without compromising their utility, as traditional methods like globally applied safety tuning often degrade model performance on benign inputs. The proposed Continuous Latent Adapter Routing (CLEAR) framework introduces a lightweight hidden-state gate that allows for continuous control over the activation of a safety low-rank adapter, thereby minimizing harmful outputs while preserving the model's response quality. This approach is well-motivated as it seeks to balance safety and utility, a critical concern in LLM alignment. The methodology involves applying CLEAR to safety and utility benchmarks, demonstrating significant improvements in robustness on HarmBench and achieving a reduction in harmful completions from 32.3% to 0.5% on Llama-3-8B-Instruct, while also enhancing GSM8K accuracy by up to 7.1 percentage points compared to traditional methods. These findings indicate that CLEAR effectively addresses the safety-utility trade-off in LLMs.
本研究解决了在不损害大型语言模型（LLMs）效用的情况下提高其安全性的问题，因为传统的安全调优方法往往会导致对良性输入的性能下降。以往的方法，如全局应用的安全调优和标准低秩适配（LoRA），在影响模型响应方面存在局限性。提出的连续潜在适配器路由（CLEAR）框架引入了一种轻量级的隐藏状态门，允许对安全低秩适配器的激活强度进行持续控制，有效减少有害输出，同时保持模型在良性提示上的性能。本文的贡献在于提供了一种新机制，改善了LLM对齐中的安全性与效用的平衡。通过在安全性和效用基准上的实验，CLEAR显示出显著的改进，将Llama-3-8B-Instruct上的HarmBench ASR从32.3%降低到0.5%，同时相比传统方法在GSM8K准确率上提高了多达7.1个百分点，从而支持其在实现所需安全性与效用权衡方面的有效性。
RouteScan: A Non-Intrusive Approach to Auditing MoE LLMs Safety via Expert Routing Telemetry
Authors: Bo Lv, Zhiheng Xu, KeDong Xiu, Ruyi Ding, Tianhang Zheng, Zhibo Wang, Kui Ren
First: 2026-05-24T02:06:44+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-21T12:55:57+00:00
Comments: 11 pages. Revised manuscript with expanded experiments
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
As Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architectures are increasingly adopted for scaling Large Language Models (LLMs), safety auditing becomes necessary to verify whether these models produce or facilitate harmful behaviors during operation. However, existing content-based auditing methods typically require access to user prompts, model internals, or outputs, potentially exposing sensitive user information and creating a tension between LLM safety and user privacy. On the other hand, we observe that, in MoE models, different inputs induce different sparse expert-routing patterns, which produce measurable footprints in low-level GPU execution telemetry. We refer to these hardware-observable signals induced by expert-routing decisions as expert routing telemetry; they are derived from GPU execution rather than from router logits or token-level routing assignments. Inspired by this observation, we propose RouteScan, a non-intrusive auditing framework for detecting harmful behaviors through such routing-induced GPU telemetry. Specifically, RouteScan utilizes the number of active GPU threads allocated to expert modules during the prefilling phase as a discriminative micro-architectural fingerprint, and builds a lightweight detection pipeline that isolates cross-domain invariant risk indicators for the precise identification of malicious prompts. Comprehensive evaluations on four open-source MoE LLMs with distinct routing designs demonstrate that RouteScan achieves strong generalization, with an AUROC exceeding 0.91 on unseen harmful domains. Moreover, privacy stress tests show that, although aggregated execution telemetry retains input-related attribute information, full prompts and exact sensitive fields cannot be reliably recovered under the evaluated attacks.
中文标题/摘要
标题：RouteScan：一种通过专家路由遥测对MoE LLMs安全进行非侵入式审计的方法
随着混合专家（MoE）架构在大规模语言模型（LLMs）中的日益采用，安全审计变得必要，以验证这些模型在操作过程中是否产生或促进有害行为。然而，现有的基于内容的审计方法通常需要访问用户提示、模型内部或输出，可能会暴露敏感用户信息，并在LLM安全性与用户隐私之间产生紧张关系。另一方面，我们观察到，在MoE模型中，不同的输入会引发不同的稀疏专家路由模式，这在低级GPU执行遥测中产生可测量的足迹。我们将这些由专家路由决策引发的硬件可观察信号称为专家路由遥测；它们源自GPU执行，而非路由器logits或令牌级路由分配。受此观察启发，我们提出了RouteScan，一种通过这种路由引发的GPU遥测检测有害行为的非侵入式审计框架。具体而言，RouteScan利用在预填充阶段分配给专家模块的活跃GPU线程数量作为区分性微架构指纹，并构建一个轻量级检测管道，隔离跨域不变风险指标，以精确识别恶意提示。对四个具有不同路由设计的开源MoE LLMs的全面评估表明，RouteScan实现了强大的泛化能力，在未见过的有害领域中AUROC超过0.91。此外，隐私压力测试表明，尽管聚合的执行遥测保留了与输入相关的属性信息，但在评估的攻击下，无法可靠恢复完整提示和确切的敏感字段。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the growing need for safety auditing in Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architectures used in Large Language Models (LLMs), which can potentially produce harmful outputs while also risking user privacy through existing content-based auditing methods. Traditional methods often require access to sensitive user data, which raises privacy concerns, while the proposed RouteScan framework leverages expert routing telemetry derived from GPU execution to non-intrusively detect harmful behaviors without compromising user information. This approach is well-motivated as it provides a solution to the conflict between LLM safety and user privacy. RouteScan employs a lightweight detection pipeline that analyzes the number of active GPU threads during the prefilling phase to identify malicious prompts based on cross-domain invariant risk indicators. Evaluations on four open-source MoE LLMs demonstrate that RouteScan achieves an AUROC exceeding 0.91 on unseen harmful domains, indicating strong generalization and effective performance in supporting its safety auditing goals while maintaining user privacy.
本研究关注于在使用混合专家（MoE）架构的大型语言模型（LLMs）中进行安全审计的日益需求，这些模型可能产生有害行为，同时由于现有的基于内容的审计方法需要访问敏感用户数据而引发隐私问题。以往的方法往往妨碍用户隐私，并未有效隔离有害行为，而所提出的RouteScan框架利用从GPU执行中获得的专家路由遥测，提供了一种非侵入式的审计解决方案。该方法的动机充分，因为它允许在不暴露用户提示或模型内部信息的情况下检测有害行为。RouteScan采用轻量级检测管道，分析在预填充阶段分配给专家模块的活动GPU线程数量，以识别恶意提示，在四种不同的MoE LLM上实现了超过0.91的AUROC分数，展示了强大的泛化能力，支持其在不妨碍隐私的情况下确保安全的目标。
Certified Multi-Turn Robustness for LLM Safety via Compositional Bounds and Safety Persistence
Authors: Yang Liu, Bin Chong, Wenkai Yang, Shuai Zhang, Yancheng Chen, Feiyu Han, GuoZhen, Cheng Zhang, Huaibing Xie, Changze Lv, Shihan Dou, Pluto Zhou
First: 2026-08-21T07:43:06+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-21T07:43:06+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) are vulnerable to multi-turn jailbreak attacks that progressively manipulate conversation context. Existing certified robustness methods are limited to single-turn inputs; naive multi-turn composition yields bounds that degrade exponentially in the number of turns. We introduce Multi-Turn Certified Robustness (MTCR), a framework that models conversational safety via State-Adversarial MDPs and defines $k$-turn certified robustness as the worst-case safety probability across $k$ adversarial turns. MTCR comprises: (i) compositional certification via embedding-space mode decomposition, yielding tighter certified lower bounds than naive multiplication; (ii) $(α,β)$-safety persistence, improving the degradation rate from $\underline{p}^{k}$ to $β^k$ (with $β> \underline{p}$) and yielding interpretable horizon estimates; (iii) matching information-theoretic upper bounds establishing tightness; and (iv) a unified algorithm combining these results. Experiments on six LLMs under $ε$-bounded and Crescendo-style attacks confirm that empirical safety consistently exceeds the certified bounds.
中文标题/摘要
标题：通过组合界限和安全持久性实现LLM安全的认证多轮鲁棒性
大型语言模型（LLMs）易受多轮越狱攻击，这些攻击逐步操控对话上下文。现有的认证鲁棒性方法仅限于单轮输入；简单的多轮组合导致界限在轮数上呈指数级下降。我们引入了多轮认证鲁棒性（MTCR），这是一个通过状态对抗MDP建模对话安全的框架，并将$k$轮认证鲁棒性定义为$k$轮对抗中的最坏情况安全概率。MTCR包括：（i）通过嵌入空间模式分解进行组合认证，产生比简单乘法更紧的认证下界；（ii）$(α,β)$-安全持久性，将降级率从$\underline{p}^{k}$改善到$β^k$（其中$β> \underline{p}$），并产生可解释的时间估计；（iii）匹配信息论上界限以建立紧性；（iv）结合这些结果的统一算法。在$ε$-有界和Crescendo风格攻击下对六个LLM的实验确认，经验安全性始终超过认证界限。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the vulnerability of large language models (LLMs) to multi-turn jailbreak attacks that exploit conversation context, highlighting the limitations of existing certified robustness methods which only apply to single-turn inputs and suffer from exponential degradation in multi-turn scenarios. The proposed Multi-Turn Certified Robustness (MTCR) framework distinguishes itself by modeling conversational safety through State-Adversarial MDPs and defining certified robustness across multiple turns, thus overcoming the shortcomings of previous approaches. The contributions of this paper include a novel compositional certification method that provides tighter bounds, an improved safety persistence mechanism, and a unified algorithm that integrates these advancements. The methodology involves rigorous testing on six LLMs against various attack types, demonstrating that the empirical safety achieved consistently surpasses the certified bounds, thereby supporting the framework's effectiveness in enhancing LLM safety.
本研究针对大型语言模型（LLMs）在多轮越狱攻击中易受操控的脆弱性，指出现有的认证鲁棒性方法仅适用于单轮输入，并且在多轮场景中存在指数级降解的问题。提出的多轮认证鲁棒性（MTCR）框架通过状态对抗马尔可夫决策过程（State-Adversarial MDPs）建模对话安全，并定义了跨多个对抗轮次的认证鲁棒性，从而克服了简单多轮组合的不足。本文的贡献在于提出了一种新方法，包括用于更紧束缚的组合认证、安全持久性以改善降解率，以及整合这些元素的统一算法。该方法在六个LLMs上进行了ε界限和Crescendo风格攻击的测试，结果表明经验安全性始终超过认证界限，支持了该框架在增强LLM安全性方面的有效性。
Calibrate-Then-Delegate: Safety Monitoring with Risk and Budget Guarantees via Model Cascades
Authors: Edoardo Pona, Milad Kazemi, Mehran Hosseini, Yali Du, David Watson, Osvaldo Simeone, Nicola Paoletti
First: 2026-04-15T11:05:59+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-21T07:17:49+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Monitoring LLM safety at scale requires balancing cost and accuracy: a cheap latent-space probe can screen every input, but hard cases should be escalated to a more expensive expert. Existing cascades delegate based on probe uncertainty, but uncertainty is a poor proxy for the utility of an expert call, as it ignores whether the expert would actually improve the prediction. To address this problem, we introduce Calibrate-Then-Delegate (CTD), a model-cascade approach that provides probabilistic guarantees on either computation cost or safety performance while enabling instance-level (streaming) decisions. CTD builds on a novel delegation value (DV) probe, a lightweight model operating on the same internal representations as the safety probe that predicts the benefit of replacing the probe score with the expert score after an expert call. CTD calibrates a threshold on the DV signal using held-out data and multiple hypothesis testing, yielding finite-sample guarantees while optimising the complementary objective. Evaluated on four safety datasets using learned-attention probes, CTD improves over uncertainty-based delegation over much of the budget range, avoids unnecessary expert calls, and extracts positive-value subsets even from groups where the expert score is worse than the probe score on average. Ablations across four probe architectures show that these findings are not specific to any particular architecture.
中文标题/摘要
标题：校准后委派：通过模型级联实现风险和预算保证的安全监测
大规模监测LLM安全性需要平衡成本和准确性：便宜的潜在空间探测器可以筛选每个输入，但困难案例应升级到更昂贵的专家。现有的级联基于探测器的不确定性进行委派，但不确定性并不是专家调用效用的良好代理，因为它忽略了专家是否真的会改善预测。为了解决这个问题，我们引入了校准后委派（CTD），这是一种模型级联方法，提供计算成本或安全性能的概率保证，同时支持实例级（流式）决策。CTD基于一种新颖的委派价值（DV）探测器，该轻量级模型在与安全探测器相同的内部表示上运行，预测在专家调用后用专家分数替代探测器分数的好处。CTD使用保留数据和多重假设检验对DV信号进行阈值校准，提供有限样本保证，同时优化互补目标。在四个安全数据集上使用学习注意力探测器进行评估，CTD在大部分预算范围内优于基于不确定性的委派，避免了不必要的专家调用，并从专家分数平均低于探测器分数的组中提取出正值子集。对四种探测器架构的消融实验表明，这些发现并不特定于任何特定架构。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the challenge of monitoring the safety of large language models (LLMs) at scale, where there is a need to balance cost and accuracy in decision-making. Previous methods relied on probe uncertainty to delegate tasks, which proved inadequate as it did not accurately reflect the utility of expert intervention. The proposed Calibrate-Then-Delegate (CTD) approach improves upon existing methods by introducing a delegation value (DV) probe that assesses the benefit of expert calls based on internal model representations, allowing for instance-level decisions with probabilistic guarantees on cost or safety. The methodology was evaluated on four safety datasets, demonstrating that CTD outperforms uncertainty-based delegation across various budget ranges, minimizes unnecessary expert calls, and effectively identifies valuable subsets even when expert scores are generally lower than probe scores, thus supporting the goal of efficient safety monitoring.
本研究解决了大规模监测大型语言模型（LLMs）安全性所面临的挑战，这需要在成本和准确性之间取得平衡。以往的方法依赖于探测器的不确定性来委派任务，但这种方法存在缺陷，因为它没有考虑专家的输入是否真的会改善预测。提出的Calibrate-Then-Delegate（CTD）方法通过引入一个委派值（DV）探测器来评估专家参与的潜在益处，从而改进了现有技术，允许实例级决策，同时确保计算成本或安全性能的概率保证。该方法在四个安全数据集上进行了评估，结果表明CTD在各种预算场景下优于基于不确定性的委派，减少了不必要的专家咨询，并能够识别出即使在专家评分通常低于探测器评分的情况下也具有价值的子集，从而支持其在实现安全监测目标方面的有效性。
aiXamine: Unified Black-Box Evaluation of Cross-Dimensional Trade-offs in LLM Safety, Security, and Privacy
Authors: Fatih Deniz, Yazan Boshmaf, Dorde Popovic, Issa Khalil
First: 2026-08-20T20:33:35+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-20T20:33:35+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
The critical failure modes in deployed large language models (LLMs) are cross-dimensional: a model can score 99.3 in safety alignment while refusing one in three benign queries, or improve across every capability metric while losing 21 points in privacy. Existing evaluation frameworks that assess safety, security, and privacy independently cannot detect these patterns. We introduce aiXamine, a unified black-box platform that evaluates LLM trustworthiness across safety, security, and privacy as interdependent properties. aiXamine orchestrates 46 tests across nine services through an automated red-teaming pipeline, producing hierarchical risk profiles, from prompt-level diagnostics to cross-service trade-off analytics, that enable reproducible comparison of proprietary and open-weight systems under identical conditions. Applying aiXamine to over 120 LLMs through more than 5,000 test runs, we conduct the largest joint safety, security, and privacy study to date and uncover three cross-dimensional phenomena invisible to single-axis evaluation. First, safety enforcement incurs a quantifiable safety tax: stronger alignment systematically increases over-refusal, forcing providers to choose between protection and utility. Second, privacy is near-orthogonal to other trustworthiness dimensions and not captured by standard alignment. Third, we identify and formally characterize distillation-induced robustness collapse: off-policy distillation without on-policy correction causes entropy collapse, catastrophically destroying robustness (56.9$\to$2.6) on the same base architecture. These findings, compounded by diminishing returns from scale and category-dependent safety behaviors, demonstrate that trustworthiness is inherently multi-dimensional: progress along one axis does not guarantee, and can actively undermine, progress along others, yet current alignment methods treat it as a single objective.
中文标题/摘要
标题：aiXamine：统一黑箱评估大型语言模型安全性、保密性和隐私中的跨维权衡
已部署的大型语言模型（LLMs）中的关键失败模式是跨维的：一个模型在安全对齐上得分99.3，但拒绝三分之一的良性查询，或者在每个能力指标上都有所提升，但隐私损失21分。现有的评估框架独立评估安全性、保密性和隐私，无法检测这些模式。我们引入了aiXamine，一个统一的黑箱平台，评估LLM在安全性、保密性和隐私方面的可信度，作为相互依赖的属性。aiXamine通过自动化红队管道协调46个测试，涵盖九项服务，生成从提示级诊断到跨服务权衡分析的分层风险概况，使在相同条件下对专有和开放权重系统的可重复比较成为可能。通过超过5000次测试运行将aiXamine应用于120多个LLM，我们进行了迄今为止最大的联合安全性、保密性和隐私研究，揭示了三种在单轴评估中不可见的跨维现象。首先，安全执行会产生可量化的安全税：更强的对齐系统性地增加过度拒绝，迫使提供者在保护和效用之间做出选择。其次，隐私与其他可信度维度几乎正交，标准对齐无法捕捉。第三，我们识别并正式表征了蒸馏引起的鲁棒性崩溃：没有政策校正的离政策蒸馏导致熵崩溃，灾难性地破坏了鲁棒性（56.9$\to$2.6）在相同的基础架构上。这些发现，加上规模的递减收益和类别依赖的安全行为，表明可信度本质上是多维的：在一个轴上的进展并不保证，并且可能会积极破坏其他轴上的进展，而当前的对齐方法将其视为单一目标。

The research addresses the critical failure modes in large language models (LLMs), which exhibit interdependent issues across safety, security, and privacy that existing evaluation frameworks fail to capture. The methodology involves automated red-teaming and extensive testing of over 120 LLMs, uncovering critical insights into the multi-dimensional nature of trustworthiness in LLMs, thus supporting the need for a more integrated evaluation approach.

Enforcing LLM Safety through DMD-based Classification of Prompt-Response Embedding Dynamics
Authors: Mohamed Akrout, Olivera Kotevska, Dan Wilson
First: 2026-08-20T02:50:44+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-20T02:50:44+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed in high-stakes applications, yet their tendency to generate toxic, harmful, or policy-violating content poses significant risks. Detecting these unsafe outputs efficiently in a black-box manner remains an open challenge. In this paper, we extend a recently proposed dynamical systems framework designed for hallucination detection to LLM safety classification. By projecting both prompts and responses into high-dimensional embedding spaces and fitting separate Koopman-based predictive models for safe and unsafe regimes, we classify new outputs using a new differential residual score that compares prediction errors of the safe and unsafe regimes. A key contribution is the incorporation of the prompt and response embedding dynamics, yielding fitted Koopman operators that capture crucial interaction patterns. We evaluate our black-box method across three safety benchmarks using three embedding models. Our results show that incorporating prompt embeddings yields consistent improvements, particularly for interaction-dependent violations when paired with causal decoders (e.g., in Llama-3), while response-only violations benefit more from dense semantic embedding representations. These findings opens the door for using dynamical systems to analyze AI systems rather than the dominant paradigm of using AI to model dynamical systems.
中文标题/摘要
标题：通过基于DMD的提示-响应嵌入动态分类来强化LLM安全性
大型语言模型（LLMs）越来越多地应用于高风险场景，但它们生成有毒、有害或违反政策的内容的倾向带来了重大风险。以黑箱方式高效检测这些不安全输出仍然是一个未解决的挑战。本文扩展了最近提出的用于幻觉检测的动态系统框架，以进行LLM安全分类。通过将提示和响应投影到高维嵌入空间，并为安全和不安全状态拟合单独的基于Koopman的预测模型，我们使用新的微分残差分数对新输出进行分类，该分数比较安全和不安全状态的预测误差。一个关键贡献是结合了提示和响应嵌入动态，得到了捕捉关键交互模式的拟合Koopman算子。我们在三个安全基准上使用三种嵌入模型评估了我们的黑箱方法。我们的结果表明，结合提示嵌入可以带来一致的改进，特别是在与因果解码器（例如Llama-3）配对时，针对交互依赖的违规行为，而仅响应的违规行为则更受密集语义嵌入表示的益处。这些发现为使用动态系统分析AI系统打开了大门，而不是使用AI建模动态系统的主流范式。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the growing concern of Large Language Models (LLMs) generating unsafe content in high-stakes applications, highlighting the challenge of efficiently detecting such outputs in a black-box manner. The proposed approach utilizes a dynamical systems framework to extend hallucination detection into LLM safety classification by projecting prompts and responses into high-dimensional embedding spaces and employing Koopman-based predictive models to differentiate between safe and unsafe outputs. This method is well-motivated as it incorporates the dynamics of prompt and response embeddings, leading to improved classification accuracy. Fair ASR: Re-Evaluating Black-Box Jailbreaks under Shared Target-Call Budgets
Authors: Zhida He, Xiaoyu Wen, Han Qi, Ziyuan Zhou, Peng Yu, Jiajia Li, Chaochao Lu, Qiaosheng Zhang
First: 2026-08-18T04:26:39+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-18T04:26:39+00:00
Comments: 29 pages, 8 figures, 13 tables. Code: https://github.com/xsddys/Fair-ASR
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Reliable jailbreak evaluation is essential for assessing LLM safety, but most existing studies rely solely on attack success rate (ASR) without accounting for its dependence on attack budgets, resulting in unfair comparisons across methods. Existing compute-aware evaluations reduce heterogeneous resources into FLOPs, which is difficult to estimate for black-box models and fails to capture resource-specific constraints. To provide a comparable evaluation basis, we introduce Fair-ASR, an evaluation protocol for black-box jailbreak attacks under shared target-call budgets B, using target calls as a directly observable and method-agnostic comparison axis while tracking attacker calls separately for efficiency analysis. We re-evaluate 11 representative attacks under the Fair-ASR protocol and find that attack rankings change substantially across target-call budgets, simple stochastic perturbations and hand-crafted templates remain highly competitive under equal target access, and no evaluated LLM-driven method is efficient in both target and attacker calls. Motivated by this efficiency gap, we introduce ReCode, a compositional budget-efficient attack that combines desensitization rewriting with two effective low-cost primitives identified by Fair-ASR. Under a budget of 20 target calls, ReCode achieves 85% ASR on GPT-5 while requiring only 7.19 attacker calls per request on average, showing strong efficiency in both target and attacker calls.
中文标题/摘要
标题：公平ASR：在共享目标调用预算下重新评估黑箱越狱
可靠的越狱评估对于评估大型语言模型（LLM）的安全性至关重要，但现有大多数研究仅依赖攻击成功率（ASR），而未考虑其对攻击预算的依赖，导致方法之间的不公平比较。现有的计算感知评估将异构资源简化为FLOPs，这对于黑箱模型难以估计，并未捕捉资源特定的约束。为了提供可比较的评估基础，我们引入了公平ASR，这是一种在共享目标调用预算B下评估黑箱越狱攻击的评估协议，使用目标调用作为直接可观察和方法无关的比较轴，同时单独跟踪攻击者调用以进行效率分析。我们在公平ASR协议下重新评估了11个代表性攻击，发现攻击排名在目标调用预算之间发生了显著变化，简单的随机扰动和手工制作的模板在平等目标访问下仍然具有高度竞争力，并且没有评估的LLM驱动方法在目标和攻击者调用中都有效率。受到这一效率差距的启发，我们引入了ReCode，这是一种组合预算高效的攻击，结合了去敏感重写和公平ASR识别的两种有效低成本原语。在20个目标调用的预算下，ReCode在GPT-5上实现了85%的ASR，同时平均每个请求仅需7.19个攻击者调用，显示出在目标和攻击者调用中的强效率。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the need for reliable evaluation of black-box jailbreak attacks on large language models (LLMs), highlighting that existing studies primarily focus on attack success rates (ASR) without considering the impact of attack budgets, leading to unfair comparisons. Previous methods have struggled with estimating compute resources for black-box models and have not effectively captured resource-specific constraints. The proposed Fair-ASR evaluation protocol introduces a method that uses shared target-call budgets for a more equitable comparison of attacks while separately analyzing attacker calls for efficiency. The paper contributes by re-evaluating 11 representative attacks under this new protocol, revealing significant changes in attack rankings based on target-call budgets, and demonstrating that no LLM-driven method is efficient in both target and attacker calls. The methodology includes the introduction of ReCode, a budget-efficient attack that combines desensitization rewriting with low-cost primitives, achieving an 85% ASR on GPT-5 with only 7.19 attacker calls per request under a budget of 20 target calls, thus supporting the goals of improved efficiency and fairness in evaluation.

Making Your LLMs More Objective: Stabilizing LLM Safety Behavior Across Traits with Trait-Invariant Safety Tuning
Authors: Lang Cao
First: 2026-08-12T06:33:55+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-12T06:33:55+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Aligned large language models (LLMs) are expected to exhibit safety behavior based on the content of the user request: they should refuse unsafe requests and comply with safe ones. However, we show that the same request can elicit substantially different safety decisions under different traits assigned in the system prompt, a failure mode we call trait-induced safety variation. To measure this failure, we introduce refusal-based metrics: Trait-Induced Deviation measures dataset-level deviation from the no-trait baseline, while Trait-Induced Flip Rate measures whether the same request receives different safety decisions across traits. We then provide a representation-level analysis of the mechanism behind trait-induced safety shifts and find that traits perturb the model's safety representations within a low-dimensional subspace. To achieve trait-invariant safety, where safety behavior remains stable across traits, we introduce Trait-Invariant Safety Tuning (TIST), a simple yet effective self-distillation framework that aligns an LLM's trait-conditioned behavior with its no-trait behavior. Guided by our analysis, we further propose Trait-Subspace Neutralization (TraSN), an instantiation of TIST, which enforces invariance only within the identified trait subspace. Experiments show that TraSN improves trait-invariant safety and strengthens harmful-request safety while preserving general capability. Our results highlight traits as an important factor in LLM safety and robust model behavior.
中文标题/摘要
标题：使您的大型语言模型更客观：通过特征不变安全调优稳定大型语言模型的安全行为
对齐的大型语言模型（LLMs）预计会根据用户请求的内容表现出安全行为：它们应该拒绝不安全的请求并遵守安全的请求。然而，我们展示了同一请求在系统提示中分配的不同特征下可能引发显著不同的安全决策，这种失败模式我们称之为特征引起的安全变异。为了衡量这种失败，我们引入了基于拒绝的指标：特征引起的偏差测量数据集级别的偏差与无特征基线的偏差，而特征引起的翻转率测量同一请求在不同特征下是否收到不同的安全决策。然后，我们提供了特征引起的安全变化机制的表示级分析，发现特征在低维子空间内扰动模型的安全表示。为了实现特征不变安全，即安全行为在不同特征间保持稳定，我们引入了特征不变安全调优（TIST），这是一种简单而有效的自蒸馏框架，将LLM的特征条件行为与其无特征行为对齐。在我们的分析指导下，我们进一步提出了特征子空间中和（TraSN），这是TIST的一种实例，强制在识别的特征子空间内保持不变。实验表明，TraSN提高了特征不变安全性，并增强了对有害请求的安全性，同时保持了整体能力。我们的结果强调特征是LLM安全性和稳健模型行为的重要因素。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the issue of inconsistent safety behavior in aligned large language models (LLMs), which can yield different safety decisions based on the traits assigned in the system prompt, a phenomenon termed trait-induced safety variation. Previous methods did not adequately account for this variability, leading to unreliable safety responses. The proposed approach, Trait-Invariant Safety Tuning (TIST), introduces a self-distillation framework that aligns the model's behavior across different traits, while Trait-Subspace Neutralization (TraSN) specifically targets the identified trait subspace to enforce safety invariance. The contribution of this paper lies in its novel metrics for measuring safety deviations and its effective methodology that enhances safety behavior without compromising the model's general capabilities. Experimental results demonstrate that TraSN significantly improves trait-invariant safety and harmful-request safety, supporting the goal of achieving more reliable LLM behavior.
本研究解决了对齐的大型语言模型（LLMs）在系统提示中分配的特征下安全行为显著不同的问题，这导致安全决策不一致。以往的方法未能充分考虑这种特征引起的安全变化，导致安全响应不可靠。提出的方法特征不变安全调优（TIST）旨在通过将模型的特征条件行为与无特征行为对齐，来稳定不同特征下的安全行为。该方法的动机源于对特征如何影响安全表示的表征级分析。本文的贡献包括引入拒绝基础指标来衡量安全变化，以及开发特征子空间中立化（TraSN），该方法在特定特征子空间内强制执行安全不变性。实验结果表明，TraSN增强了特征不变安全性，提高了对有害请求的响应，同时保持了模型的整体能力，支持了在特征间实现一致安全行为的目标。
ProbGuard: Calibrated Safety Risk Estimation from LLM Output Distributions
Authors: Xinzhe Huang, Biwu Yao, Kedong Xiu, Mengnan Zhao, Di Wang, Puning Zhao, Tianhang Zheng
First: 2026-08-11T08:08:41+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-11T08:08:41+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Recent research on Large Language Model (LLM) safety has widely adopted guardrails to identify unsafe LLM outputs. Existing guardrails typically formulate safety assessment as a deterministic classification task, mapping a discrete token sequence to a discrete safety label. However, this paradigm has two limitations: First, safety assessment is inherently an uncertain problem, particularly during the early generation state. Second, relying solely on discrete token sequences discards the rich probabilistic information embedded in the LLM output distribution. To address these limitations, we propose the first completely probabilistic architecture-agnostic guardrail \textsc{ProbGuard} to leverage the LLM early output distributional signals for estimating and calibrating the safety probability, thereby enabling early stopping of unsafe ongoing outputs. Specifically, given an LLM's generated prefix distribution, we formulate the safety risk as the unsafe probability of its continued generation dynamics and estimate this risk by Monte-Carlo sampling. Through post-training on the distributional signals and calibrated safety risk, \textsc{ProbGuard} achieves the best calibration performance across all nine model--dataset combination settings, reducing the average Brier score and ECE by 79.6\% and 71.9\%, respectively, over the best baseline. \textsc{ProbGuard} further limits the attack success rate to at most 1\% across six representative jailbreak attacks after observing the LLM early output distributions from only the first ten decoding steps.
中文标题/摘要
标题：ProbGuard：基于LLM输出分布的校准安全风险估计
近期对大型语言模型（LLM）安全性的研究广泛采用了保护措施来识别不安全的LLM输出。现有的保护措施通常将安全评估表述为一个确定性的分类任务，将离散的标记序列映射到离散的安全标签。然而，这一范式存在两个局限性：首先，安全评估本质上是一个不确定性问题，特别是在早期生成阶段。其次，仅依赖离散标记序列会丢弃嵌入在LLM输出分布中的丰富概率信息。为了解决这些局限性，我们提出了第一个完全概率架构无关的保护措施\textsc{ProbGuard}，利用LLM早期输出的分布信号来估计和校准安全概率，从而实现对不安全持续输出的早期停止。具体而言，给定LLM生成的前缀分布，我们将安全风险表述为其持续生成动态的不安全概率，并通过蒙特卡洛采样来估计这一风险。通过对分布信号和校准安全风险的后训练，\textsc{ProbGuard}在所有九个模型-数据集组合设置中实现了最佳的校准性能，平均Brier分数和ECE分别减少了79.6\%和71.9\%，超越了最佳基线。\textsc{ProbGuard}进一步将六个代表性越狱攻击中的攻击成功率限制在最多1\%。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the limitations of existing safety assessment methods for Large Language Models (LLMs), which typically treat safety evaluation as a deterministic classification task, neglecting the inherent uncertainty and rich probabilistic information in LLM output distributions. The proposed approach, ProbGuard, is a probabilistic architecture-agnostic guardrail that utilizes early output distribution signals to estimate and calibrate safety probabilities, allowing for early termination of unsafe outputs. The contribution of this paper lies in its novel methodology that employs Monte-Carlo sampling to assess safety risk based on the LLM's generated prefix distribution. ProbGuard demonstrates superior calibration performance across nine model-dataset combinations, achieving a 79.6% reduction in average Brier score and a 71.9% reduction in expected calibration error compared to the best baseline, while also limiting attack success rates to 1% across six jailbreak scenarios after analyzing early output distributions.

Agent Safety Should Be a Runtime Contract
Authors: Albus W. Ng, Yi Han, Jusheng Zhang, Wenhao Wang
First: 2026-08-11T08:01:05+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-11T08:01:05+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
The dominant paradigm treats AI safety as a property to be instilled during model training via RLHF, DPO, or Constitutional AI. We argue this is structurally insufficient for autonomous agents that execute code, mutate files, send messages, and modify databases. Agent safety should be a runtime contract enforced by the harness, and the contract has two complementary faces. The preventive face blocks dangerous actions before they happen via sandboxes, permission gates, output filters, and trajectory monitors. The evidential face requires verifiable proof that good actions actually happened, gating task submission on hard evidence such as test runs, log captures, file diffs, and citation grounding. We ground the position in four lines of public evidence, with row-level protocols and data released in the supplementary JSON files: a survey of 52 documented AI-agent and LLM safety incidents, a false-completion audit with 31 non-contested core cases plus one disputed illustrative case, a trajectory-schema audit of 12 public agent systems and harnesses, and a title-level audit of all 28,560 papers accepted at NeurIPS, ICML, and ICLR 2023-2025 showing a pooled 8-12x imbalance between training-time and deployment-time publication. Two prior communities that needed to enforce safety, computer security and the experimental sciences, converged on runtime contracts with both preventive and evidential elements; agentic AI is now under the same pressure. We formalize an Agent Trajectory Schema and Evidence Chain, state a compositional gating proposition based on standard monitor composition, and outline a research agenda. The right unit of safety in agentic AI is the trajectory-with-checkable-evidence, not the model.
中文标题/摘要
标题：代理安全应为运行时契约
主流范式将AI安全视为在模型训练期间通过RLHF、DPO或宪法AI灌输的属性。我们认为这对执行代码、修改文件、发送消息和修改数据库的自主代理来说结构上是不够的。代理安全应由安全框架强制执行的运行时契约，该契约有两个互补的方面。预防性方面通过沙箱、权限门、输出过滤器和轨迹监控在危险行为发生之前阻止它们。证据性方面要求可验证的证据证明良好行为确实发生，任务提交需基于硬证据，如测试运行、日志捕获、文件差异和引用基础。我们在四条公共证据中支持这一立场，附加JSON文件中发布了行级协议和数据：对52起记录的AI代理和LLM安全事件的调查、31个无争议核心案例加一个有争议示例案例的虚假完成审计、12个公共代理系统和安全框架的轨迹模式审计，以及对2023-2025年NeurIPS、ICML和ICLR接受的28,560篇论文的标题级审计，显示训练时间和部署时间出版之间的8-12倍不平衡。两个需要强制执行安全的先前社区，计算机安全和实验科学，已在预防性和证据性元素的运行时契约上达成共识；代理AI现在也面临同样的压力。我们形式化了代理轨迹模式和证据链，基于标准监控组合提出了一个组合门控命题，并概述了研究议程。代理AI中的安全正确单位是带可检查证据的轨迹，而不是模型。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the inadequacies of current AI safety paradigms, which primarily focus on instilling safety during model training through methods like RLHF and DPO, but fail to account for the complexities of autonomous agents that perform actions such as executing code and modifying databases. The proposed approach shifts the focus to enforcing agent safety as a runtime contract, incorporating both preventive measures to block dangerous actions and evidential requirements to verify that safe actions have occurred. This method is motivated by the need for a more robust safety framework, drawing parallels with established practices in computer security and experimental sciences. The paper contributes by formalizing an Agent Trajectory Schema and Evidence Chain, proposing a compositional gating proposition, and outlining a research agenda. The methodology emphasizes the importance of checkable evidence in agentic AI safety, and the findings suggest that this approach could significantly enhance safety measures in real-world applications of AI agents.

HoloAegis: Frozen Representation, Topological Inference: Minimally Parametric Safety Manifolds for Zero-Shot LLM Guardrails
Authors: Tak Ho Alex Li, Kaijie Liu, Lik-Hang Lee, Kin Chung Ho, Ping Shum, Michael K. Ng
First: 2026-08-09T05:05:12+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-09T05:05:12+00:00
Comments: Preprint, August 2026. 10 tables, 2 figures
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Current LLM safety guardrails face a fundamental tension: fine-tuning distorts pre-trained representations while generative judges incur prohibitive inference costs. We challenge the prevailing paradigm by asking: can safety be achieved through pure geometric reasoning over frozen semantic representations? We present HoloAegis, a minimally parametric topological inference framework that decouples representation from reasoning. We term our approach minimally parametric because the only free parameters are the anchor count K and the temperature tau, both fixed after construction and requiring no gradient-based training. An un-fine-tuned encoder maps text to a unit sphere, after which all decisions are purely geometric. We formalize safety evaluation as a Gibbs-Boltzmann Free Energy computation over a pre-computed System Topology Anchor Bank, and we introduce Dual Time-Scale Exponential Moving Averages to detect progressive multi-turn semantic drift. Our key theoretical insight is a Topological Boundary Stability Conjecture: we provide theoretical motivation and strong empirical evidence that sparse anchor centroids stabilize the decision boundary against high-frequency lexical perturbations far better than full vector space methods. Evaluated across 8 benchmarks, HoloAegis achieves state-of-the-art accuracy (1.0000 AUC on AuthenHallu, 0.9802 on HarmBench) with sub-millisecond latency, zero cold-start data, and cross-lingual transfer (0.9758 AUC on Chinese CHIFRAUD).
中文标题/摘要
标题：HoloAegis：冻结表示、拓扑推理：零样本LLM安全防护的最小参数安全流形
当前LLM安全防护面临根本性矛盾：微调扭曲了预训练表示，而生成性判断则产生了高昂的推理成本。我们通过提出一个问题来挑战现有范式：是否可以通过对冻结语义表示的纯几何推理来实现安全？我们提出HoloAegis，一个最小参数的拓扑推理框架，将表示与推理解耦。我们称这种方法为最小参数，因为唯一的自由参数是锚点数量K和温度tau，构建后均为固定值且无需基于梯度的训练。未微调的编码器将文本映射到单位球体，之后所有决策均为纯几何。我们将安全评估形式化为对预计算系统拓扑锚库的Gibbs-Boltzmann自由能计算，并引入双时间尺度指数移动平均来检测渐进的多轮语义漂移。我们的关键理论见解是拓扑边界稳定性猜想：我们提供了理论动机和强有力的实证证据，表明稀疏锚心在抵御高频词汇扰动方面比全向量空间方法更能稳定决策边界。在8个基准测试中评估，HoloAegis实现了最先进的准确率（AuthenHallu上1.0000 AUC，HarmBench上0.9802），延迟低于毫秒，零冷启动数据，以及跨语言迁移（在中文CHIFRAUD上0.9758 AUC）。
Summary / 总结
This paper addresses the challenge of ensuring safety in large language models (LLMs) without distorting pre-trained representations or incurring high inference costs associated with generative judges. Previous methods often relied on fine-tuning, which compromises the integrity of the original model, or complex generative approaches that are computationally expensive. The proposed HoloAegis framework introduces a minimally parametric topological inference method that utilizes frozen semantic representations, allowing for geometric reasoning without gradient-based training. The contribution lies in its theoretical foundation, particularly the Topological Boundary Stability Conjecture, which demonstrates that sparse anchor centroids enhance decision boundary stability against lexical variations. HoloAegis achieves state-of-the-art performance across eight benchmarks, including a perfect AUC score on AuthenHallu and high scores on other tasks, while maintaining low latency and facilitating cross-lingual transfer, thus supporting its goals effectively.

Yesterday's Shield, Today's Spear: A Self-Evolving Safety Guardrail in Production
Authors: Cong Ming, Jingyi Chen, Bin Liu, Qi Chu, Tao Gong, Nenghai Yu, Yingfei Xiang
First: 2026-08-09T04:31:05+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-09T04:31:05+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Deployed LLM safety guardrails are predominantly static: trained once and frozen at release, while new jailbreak techniques and previously un-addressed harmful categories emerge within days, leaving the defense perpetually a step behind. We present SESG (Self-Evolving Safety Guardrails), a multi-agent system running in production. SESG monitors the live traffic behind a deployed guardrail and surfaces two classes of failure: jailbreaks novel in form and harmful categories novel in content. Once a failure is confirmed, a generation agent synthesizes paired training data targeted at it; a validation agent rebalances the batch toward the direction in which the deployed model errs, so that the model's own mistakes steer its training set; and a routing agent matches the training action to the diagnosed gap and returns the next version to production. Over six rounds of live evolution (V0 to V6), a 1.7B guardrail adapts to a new threat in 16-24 hours, with about 2 hours of human effort, versus the 40-90 hours of the manual process it replaces. On six emerging threats, it outperforms static guardrails from 0.6B to 9B and an adaptive baseline while preserving its general screening competence. Since April 2026, SESG has been the primary update pipeline of Sangfor's guardrail, autonomously closing 14 of 15 new threat scenarios in two months. We release 9 test sets for the 6 new threats at https://github.com/Trams1017/SESG. Warning: This paper contains examples that may be harmful or offensive.
中文标题/摘要
标题：昨日的护盾，今日的矛：生产中的自我演化安全护栏
部署的LLM安全护栏主要是静态的：训练一次后在发布时冻结，而新的越狱技术和之前未解决的有害类别在几天内出现，使防御始终滞后。我们提出了SESG（自我演化安全护栏），这是一个在生产中运行的多智能体系统。SESG监控部署护栏后面的实时流量，并揭示两类失败：形式新颖的越狱和内容新颖的有害类别。一旦确认失败，生成代理合成针对该失败的配对训练数据；验证代理将批次重新平衡到部署模型出错的方向，以便模型自身的错误引导其训练集；路由代理将训练动作与诊断出的差距匹配，并将下一个版本返回生产。在六轮实时演化（V0到V6）中，一个17亿参数的护栏在16-24小时内适应新威胁，约需2小时的人力，而替代的手动过程需40-90小时。在六个新兴威胁中，它的表现优于从6亿到90亿的静态护栏和自适应基线，同时保持其一般筛选能力。自2026年4月以来，SESG已成为Sangfor护栏的主要更新管道，在两个月内自主关闭15个新威胁场景中的14个。我们在https://github.com/Trams1017/SESG发布了6个新威胁的9个测试集。警告：本文包含可能有害或冒犯的示例。

Previous methods involve manual updates that are time-consuming and inefficient, taking 40-90 hours, while the proposed Self-Evolving Safety Guardrails (SESG) system introduces a multi-agent approach that autonomously adapts to new threats within 16-24 hours with only 2 hours of human effort. The contribution of this paper lies in its innovative methodology that includes monitoring live traffic, synthesizing targeted training data, and dynamically updating the model based on its own errors. SESG has demonstrated superior performance against six emerging threats, outperforming static guardrails and an adaptive baseline while maintaining general screening capabilities, thus effectively supporting its goal of timely and efficient threat mitigation in production environments.

Automating Deception: Scalable Multi-Turn LLM Jailbreaks
Authors: Adarsh Kumarappan, Ananya Mujoo
First: 2025-11-24T03:15:11+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-08T15:49:06+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Multi-turn conversational attacks, which leverage psychological principles like Foot-in-the-Door (FITD), where a small initial request paves the way for a more significant one, to bypass safety alignments, pose a persistent threat to Large Language Models (LLMs). Progress in defending against these attacks is hindered by a reliance on manual, hard-to-scale dataset creation. This paper introduces a novel, automated pipeline for generating large-scale, psychologically-grounded multi-turn jailbreak datasets. We systematically operationalize FITD techniques into reproducible templates, creating a benchmark of 1,500 scenarios across illegal activities and offensive content. We evaluate seven models from three major LLM families under both multi-turn (with history) and single-turn (without history) conditions. Our results reveal stark differences in contextual robustness: models in the GPT family demonstrate a significant vulnerability to conversational history, with Attack Success Rates (ASR) increasing by as much as 32 percentage points. In contrast, Google's Gemini 2.5 Flash exhibits exceptional resilience, proving nearly immune to these attacks, while Anthropic's Claude 3 Haiku shows strong but imperfect resistance. These findings highlight a critical divergence in how current safety architectures handle conversational context and underscore the need for defenses that can resist narrative-based manipulation.
中文标题/摘要
标题：自动化欺骗：可扩展的多轮LLM越狱
多轮对话攻击利用心理学原理，如“脚踏实地”（FITD），通过小的初始请求为更大的请求铺平道路，以绕过安全对齐，对大型语言模型（LLM）构成持续威胁。防御这些攻击的进展受到依赖于手动、难以扩展的数据集创建的阻碍。本文介绍了一种新颖的自动化管道，用于生成大规模、基于心理学的多轮越狱数据集。我们系统地将FITD技术操作化为可重复的模板，创建了一个包含1500个场景的基准，涵盖非法活动和攻击性内容。我们在多轮（带历史）和单轮（无历史）条件下评估了来自三个主要LLM家族的七个模型。我们的结果揭示了上下文鲁棒性方面的显著差异：GPT家族的模型对对话历史表现出显著脆弱性，攻击成功率（ASR）增加了多达32个百分点。相比之下，谷歌的Gemini 2.5 Flash表现出卓越的韧性，几乎对这些攻击免疫，而Anthropic的Claude 3 Haiku则显示出强但不完美的抵抗力。这些发现突显了当前安全架构在处理对话上下文方面的关键分歧，并强调了需要能够抵御叙事操控的防御措施。

Previous methods have relied on manual dataset creation, which is not scalable and limits the effectiveness of defenses against such attacks. The proposed approach automates the generation of large-scale, psychologically-informed multi-turn jailbreak datasets, operationalizing FITD techniques into reproducible templates and creating a benchmark of 1,500 scenarios. The methodology involves evaluating seven models from three major LLM families under both multi-turn and single-turn conditions, revealing significant differences in their contextual robustness. CASA: Classification Augmented with Safety Attention for Robust Multimodal Alignment
Authors: Anurag Kumar, Raghuveer Peri, Jon Burnsky, Alexandru Nelus, Rohit Paturi, Srikanth Vishnubhotla, Yanjun Qi
First: 2026-03-31T23:19:50+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-06T22:04:31+00:00
Comments: 9 pages + Appendix section
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Multimodal large-language models (MLLMs) often experience degraded safety alignment when harmful queries exploit cross-modal interactions. Models aligned on text alone show a higher rate of successful attacks when extended to two or more modalities. We propose a simple conditional decoding strategy, CASA (Classification Augmented with Safety Attention) that uses internal representations of MLLMs to predict a binary safety token before response generation. The classification is guided by a safety attention mechanism that computes attention between a dedicated query prompt and the input to scale the classification logits, sharpening the model's ability to detect malicious queries. Our design ensures robust safety alignment without relying on any external classifier or auxiliary head, and without modality-specific safety fine-tuning. Unlike prior safety-decoding methods that attach an external classifier or moderation head, or rescale every generated token with a reward model, CASA makes a single, internal, attention-gated safety decision over the shared multimodal representation, which is precisely what lets safety learned from text-only training transfer to image and audio inputs. Compared against 4 baseline models across 7 attack types spanning text, image, and audio modalities, evaluated on benchmarks such as MM-SafetyBench, JailbreakV-28k, and adversarial audio tests, CASA lowers the average attack success rate by >90% relative when paired with a standard safety prompt (applied to all baselines for fair comparison), and by 54--61% using its internal safety mechanism alone. Our empirical evaluations also show that CASA~maintains strong utility on benign inputs, validated through both automated and human evaluations (via 13 trained annotators). Together, these results highlight CASA as a simple and generalizable framework to improve multimodal LLM safety.

标题：CASA：增强安全注意力的分类用于稳健的多模态对齐
多模态大语言模型（MLLMs）在有害查询利用跨模态交互时，往往会出现安全对齐下降的问题。仅基于文本对齐的模型在扩展到两个或更多模态时，成功攻击的比率更高。我们提出了一种简单的条件解码策略CASA（增强安全注意力的分类），该策略利用MLLMs的内部表示在生成响应之前预测二元安全标记。分类由安全注意力机制引导，该机制计算专用查询提示与输入之间的注意力，以缩放分类logits，增强模型检测恶意查询的能力。我们的设计确保了稳健的安全对齐，无需依赖任何外部分类器或辅助头，也无需特定模态的安全微调。与之前的安全解码方法不同，CASA在共享的多模态表示上做出单一的内部注意力门控安全决策，这正是使得从仅文本训练中学习的安全性能够转移到图像和音频输入的原因。与4个基线模型在7种攻击类型（涵盖文本、图像和音频模态）进行比较，评估基准包括MM-SafetyBench、JailbreakV-28k和对抗音频测试，CASA在与标准安全提示配对时（对所有基线进行公平比较）将平均攻击成功率降低了>90%，仅使用其内部安全机制时降低了54%至61%。我们的实证评估还表明，CASA在良性输入上保持强大的实用性，通过13名训练过的注释者进行的自动化和人工评估得到了验证。这些结果共同突显了CASA作为一个简单且可推广的框架，以提高多模态LLM的安全性。

Previous methods relied on external classifiers or moderation heads, which limited their effectiveness and adaptability across modalities. The proposed CASA (Classification Augmented with Safety Attention) approach introduces a conditional decoding strategy that utilizes internal representations to predict a binary safety token before generating responses, guided by a safety attention mechanism that enhances the model's ability to detect malicious queries without the need for external components. Item Response Theory for AI Safety
Authors: Joshua Fonseca Rivera, Neil Shah, David Demitri Africa, Konstantinos Voudouris
First: 2026-08-05T17:25:27+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-05T17:25:27+00:00
Comments: 15 pages, 9 figures, 6 tables
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Language models differ in how safely they behave and these differences are measured by safety benchmarks. But aggregated benchmark scores are hard to trust and interpret, because benchmarks duplicate one another, correlate heavily, and models may sandbag when they detect evaluation. To address these issues, we draw on Item Response Theory (IRT), a statistical toolkit for measuring these latents from performance on items with inferred psychometric properties. We fit IRT models to eight safety benchmarks across 192 language models, the largest psychometric analysis of LLM safety evaluations to date, and contribute three results. First, we find that three interpretable factors of refusal strictness, truthfulness, and contextual harm explain most of the variance between models across benchmarks. Second, psychometrically selected items recover full benchmark scores with lower error than random subsets of the same size, and roughly ten adaptively chosen items suffice for several individual benchmarks, cutting evaluation cost by 97-99%. Third, IRT supports audits of individual models, showing that it can be used to detect naive sandbagging and changes of model behind APIs. Overall, we show IRT is a ready-made toolkit for reading, reducing, and auditing safety benchmarks, which we recommend frontier labs and evaluators adopt.

标题：人工智能安全的项目反应理论
语言模型在安全行为上存在差异，这些差异通过安全基准进行测量。但聚合的基准分数难以信任和解释，因为基准之间存在重复、强相关，并且模型在检测到评估时可能会故意降低表现。为了解决这些问题，我们借鉴了项目反应理论（IRT），这是一种通过对具有推断心理测量特性的项目表现进行测量的统计工具包。我们对192个语言模型的八个安全基准拟合IRT模型，这是迄今为止对大型语言模型安全评估的最大心理测量分析，并贡献了三个结果。首先，我们发现拒绝严格性、真实性和上下文伤害这三个可解释因素解释了模型在基准之间的大部分方差。其次，心理测量选择的项目以比随机子集更低的误差恢复完整的基准分数，且大约十个自适应选择的项目足以满足几个单独的基准，将评估成本降低了97-99%。第三，IRT支持对单个模型的审计，表明它可以用于检测幼稚的故意降低表现和API背后模型的变化。总体而言，我们展示了IRT是一个现成的工具包，用于解读、减少和审计安全基准，我们建议前沿实验室和评估者采用。

Robust General Utility for Reinforcement Learning
Authors: Zixuan Liu, Fangzheng Wu, Brian Summa, Zizhan Zheng
First: 2026-08-04T12:28:39+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-04T12:28:39+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Reinforcement learning (RL) with general utility extends classic RL by optimizing an arbitrary utility functional of the policy-induced occupancy measure, thereby enabling a broader range of applications. However, previous work on general utility RL typically assumes the evaluation utility is fixed and correctly specified. In practice, the utility used at deployment can deviate from the training one, creating a robustness gap that prior work does not address. Motivated by this, we propose robust general-utility RL, a minimax learning framework that trains policies against utility misspecification within a prescribed uncertainty set. Our framework strictly generalizes standard general-utility RL while also providing a unified view of many existing RL frameworks, including reward-robust RL and constrained RL, through appropriate choices of the utility uncertainty set. We further develop provably convergent stochastic algorithms for two regimes. For concave utilities, we develop a projected stochastic gradient descent-ascent method and establish stationarity guarantees. For the more challenging nonconcave regime, we propose a stochastic prox-extragradient algorithm that mitigates ill-posed behavior induced by nonconcavity, with convergence guarantees to approximate first-order stationarity. Experiments on LLM safety alignment and exploration maximization tasks further corroborate the convergence behavior consistent with our theory.
中文标题/摘要
标题：强化学习的鲁棒一般效用
具有一般效用的强化学习（RL）通过优化策略引起的占用测度的任意效用泛函，扩展了经典RL，从而使其能够应用于更广泛的场景。然而，以往关于一般效用RL的研究通常假设评估效用是固定且正确指定的。在实践中，部署时使用的效用可能与训练时的效用有所偏离，造成了以往研究未能解决的鲁棒性差距。基于此，我们提出了鲁棒一般效用RL，这是一种最小最大学习框架，旨在针对规定的不确定性集内的效用误指定训练策略。我们的框架严格推广了标准的一般效用RL，同时通过适当选择效用不确定性集，提供了许多现有RL框架（包括奖励鲁棒RL和约束RL）的统一视角。我们进一步为两种情况开发了可证明收敛的随机算法。对于凹效用，我们开发了一种投影随机梯度下降-上升方法，并建立了平稳性保证。对于更具挑战性的非凹情况，我们提出了一种随机近似外梯度算法，减轻了非凹性引起的不良行为，并保证收敛以近似一阶平稳性。在LLM安全对齐和探索最大化任务上的实验进一步证实了与我们理论一致的收敛行为。

The paper contributes by developing stochastic algorithms that ensure convergence for both concave and nonconcave utility functions, with guarantees for stationarity in the learning process. The methodology is tested on tasks related to large language model safety alignment and exploration maximization, demonstrating performance improvements that align with the theoretical expectations of the proposed approach.

EduZone: A Framework for Evaluating LLM Safety for K-12 Students and Teachers
Authors: Junyeong Park, Jieun Han, Haneul Yoo, So-Yeon Ahn, Jinsung Yoon, Alice Oh
First: 2026-08-03T10:23:42+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-03T10:23:42+00:00
Comments: Under Review
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used across diverse tasks in K-12 education, yet existing safety evaluations rarely examine how harmful or inappropriate content appears in interactions between LLMs and students or teachers. To address this, we present EduZone, an evaluation framework for LLM safety across diverse educational scenarios. Our framework systematically combines (1) student- and teacher-facing LLM usage contexts, (2) fine-grained curriculum concepts, and (3) 6 risk categories and 28 subcategories spanning both conventional and education-specific harms to generate contextually grounded adversarial interactions. We construct these interactions in three settings: single-turn requests, static multi-turn conversations, and dynamic multi-turn conversations. Using these interactions, we evaluate ten LLMs using four safety levels: refusal, safe assistance, risky assistance with safety guidance, and fully risky assistance. Our results reveal greater vulnerability to education-specific risks and dynamic multi-turn interactions, while existing safety guardrails fail to adequately address these risks. EduZone advances LLM safety in education by providing an automated, scalable evaluation framework that supports the development and deployment of safer LLMs in K-12 education.

Single Canonical Prompts Underestimate LLM Safety's Surface-Form Sensitivity
Authors: Yongxi Zhou, Junwei Yao, Yuanzhe Liu, Zihan Dong, Wenbo Ye, Jiaxi Wen, Lai Yun Choi
First: 2026-08-01T22:28:31+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-01T22:28:31+00:00
Comments: 9 pages, 3 tables
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
A benchmark score is a measurement instrument, yet most benchmarks read each item at a single canonical surface form. We ask whether that reading is faithful: when an item's intent is held fixed and only its meaning-preserving surface form varies, does the canonical-form score estimate model behavior well, and how much of any variation is decoding/judge noise rather than signal? We instantiate this in safety, a high-stakes setting with no gold label to average toward. To avoid prior confounds, we pre-author the reformulations (refusal-free, mostly non-LLM: machine back-translation and a Matrix-Language-Frame code-switch generator) so an identical surface form reaches every model, score all responses with one human-anchored, vendor-neutral judge (Claude, kappa = 0.86 vs. human on unsafe compliance, stable across languages, cross-checked by GPT-4o), and verify intent preservation. On 370 seeds x 5 surface forms x 5 models, no single transformation is uniformly most dangerous (6 of 20 per-transformation McNemar tests survive correction, most protective). Yet evaluating only the canonical prompt underestimates unsafe compliance: the union of unsafe outcomes across forms exceeds even the worst single form by 3.3-12.9 pp, with bootstrap 95% CIs excluding zero for all five models, and 5-13% of seeds safe on canonical are unsafe under some reformulation -- above a zero stochasticity floor (canonical resampled five times at temperature 0 gives 0/370 new exposures). The size of this gap is model-dependent (largest on Gemini 2.5 Pro). One form recovers only ~53% of a model's observed unsafe surface and about three reach 85% -- a redundancy characterization of this form set, not of a defined population. A benign control (XSTest) suggests the instability is bidirectional, though the benign and harmful pools are not item-matched. We release the dataset, code, and per-response labels.
中文标题/摘要
标题：单一规范提示低估了大型语言模型安全性的表面形式敏感性
基准分数是一种测量工具，但大多数基准以单一规范表面形式读取每个项目。我们询问这种读取是否忠实：当一个项目的意图保持不变，仅其保持意义的表面形式变化时，规范形式分数是否能很好地估计模型行为，以及任何变化中有多少是解码/评判噪声而非信号？我们在安全性这一高风险环境中实例化这一点，因其没有金标准可供平均。为了避免先前的混淆，我们预先授权了重构（无拒绝，主要非大型语言模型：机器回译和矩阵语言框架代码切换生成器），以便相同的表面形式到达每个模型，使用一个人类锚定的、中立的评判者（Claude，kappa = 0.86与人类在不安全合规性上的比较，在各语言中稳定，由GPT-4o交叉验证），并验证意图保持。在370个种子x 5个表面形式x 5个模型中，没有单一变换是普遍最危险的（20个每变换的McNemar测试中有6个在校正后存活，大多数是保护性的）。然而，仅评估规范提示低估了不安全合规性：跨形式的不安全结果的并集甚至超过了最糟糕的单一形式3.3-12.9个百分点，所有五个模型的自助法95%置信区间均不包括零，且5-13%的在规范下安全的种子在某些重构下是不安全的——高于零随机性底线（规范在温度0下重采样五次给出0/370个新暴露）。这一差距的大小依赖于模型（在Gemini 2.5 Pro上最大）。一种形式仅恢复了模型观察到的不安全表面的约53%，大约三种形式达到了85%——这是对这一形式集的冗余特征，而非定义人口的特征。一个良性对照（XSTest）表明不稳定性是双向的，尽管良性和有害池并未按项目匹配。我们发布了数据集、代码和每个响应标签。

This research addresses the limitations of existing benchmark scores in evaluating large language models (LLMs) by questioning the reliability of single canonical surface forms in estimating model behavior, particularly in safety contexts where no gold standard exists. Previous methods often relied on fixed prompts, which may not capture the full spectrum of model responses, leading to underestimations of unsafe compliance. Quality-Diversity Red-Teaming: Automated Generation of High-Quality and Diverse Attackers for Large Language Models
Authors: Ren-Jian Wang, Ke Xue, Zeyu Qin, Ziniu Li, Sheng Tang, Hao-Tian Li, Shengcai Liu, Zhi Yu, Yuanpeng Tan, Chao Qian
First: 2025-06-08T13:07:41+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-01T10:21:24+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Ensuring the safety and robustness of large language models (LLMs) is a fundamental challenge and a critical prerequisite for the responsible deployment of artificial intelligence. Red-teaming, a systematic framework to identify adversarial prompts that elicit harmful responses from target LLMs, has emerged as a crucial safety evaluation paradigm. Within this framework, the diversity of adversarial prompts is critical for a comprehensive safety assessment. However, previous red-teaming approaches often pursue diversity through simplistic metrics such as word frequency or sentence embedding similarity, which may not capture meaningful variation in attack strategies. In addition, the common practice of training a single attacker model restricts coverage across all potential attack styles and risk categories. This paper introduces Quality-Diversity Red-Teaming (QDRT), a new framework designed to address these limitations. QDRT achieves goal-driven diversity through behavior-conditioned training and implements a behavioral replay buffer in an open-ended manner. In addition, it trains multiple specialized attackers capable of generating high-quality attacks across diverse styles and risk categories. Our empirical evaluation demonstrates that QDRT generates attacks that are both more diverse and more effective against a wide range of target LLMs, including open-source models \texttt{GPT-2}, \texttt{Llama-3}, \texttt{Gemma-2}, \texttt{Qwen2.5}, and commercial models \texttt{GPT-4.1} and \texttt{GPT-5-Chat}. This work advances the field of LLM safety by providing a systematic and effective approach to automated red-teaming, ultimately supporting the responsible deployment of LLMs. Our code is available at https://github.com/lamda-bbo/QDRT.

EvalSafetyGap: A Hybrid Survey and Conceptual Framework for LLM Evaluation-Safety Failures
Authors: Buğra Alperen Uluırmak, Rifat Kurban
First: 2026-06-29T12:33:06+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-31T12:14:44+00:00
Comments: 74 pages, 2 figures, 4 tables. Hybrid systematic survey and conceptual framework on LLM evaluation and AI-safety failures, synthesizing 373 primary studies (2018-2026). Introduces the EvalSafetyGap framework (Instability Decomposition, Alignment Trilemma) and reports an exploratory ten-model audit. Submitted as a review/survey article; not currently under consideration elsewhere
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
This paper presents a systematic survey and conceptual synthesis of the shared measurement problem underlying large language model (LLM) evaluation and AI safety: benchmark scores, reward signals, and safety metrics can improve while the capabilities and alignment properties they are meant to represent remain uncertain. Synthesizing 373 primary studies published between 2018 and 2026, the survey organizes evidence on benchmark validity, contamination, dynamic evaluation, LLM-as-a-judge protocols, adversarial safety testing, reward and proxy optimization, mechanistic interpretability, and AI governance into an eight-stream evidence taxonomy. Building on this synthesis, we introduce EvalSafetyGap, a conceptual framework that unifies benchmark-validity and alignment-failure research as a shared proxy-target divergence problem under optimization pressure, formalized through a Goodhart-inspired Instability Decomposition and an Alignment Trilemma. An exploratory ten-model public-evidence audit illustrates the framework by showing why capability, behavioral robustness, and governance disclosure should be reported as separate evidence layers rather than collapsed into a single safety score. The survey closes with a research agenda for dynamic and contamination-resistant benchmarks, pre-specified multi-attempt threat models, version-locked evaluation, transparent source reporting, and validated mechanistic safety indicators, offering researchers, model developers, and AI auditors a shared vocabulary for measurement-aware LLM safety evaluation.

本文系统性地调查和概念性综合了大型语言模型（LLM）评估和人工智能安全的共享测量问题：基准分数、奖励信号和安全指标可以改善，而它们所代表的能力和对齐属性仍然不确定。通过综合2018年至2026年间发表的373项主要研究，调查将基准有效性、污染、动态评估、LLM作为评判者的协议、对抗性安全测试、奖励和代理优化、机制可解释性以及人工智能治理的证据组织成八个流的证据分类法。在此基础上，我们引入了EvalSafetyGap，一个概念框架，将基准有效性和对齐失败研究统一为在优化压力下的共享代理-目标偏差问题，通过受Goodhart启发的不稳定性分解和对齐三难问题进行形式化。一个探索性的十模型公共证据审计通过展示能力、行为鲁棒性和治理披露为何应作为独立的证据层而非合并为单一安全分数来说明该框架。调查最后提出了一个研究议程，涉及动态和抗污染基准、预先指定的多次尝试威胁模型、版本锁定评估、透明的来源报告和经过验证的机制安全指标，为研究人员、模型开发者和人工智能审计师提供了一个共享的测量意识的LLM安全评估词汇。

On-Policy Distillation for LLM Safety: A Routing Approach to Template-Robust Realignment
Authors: Yongjian Guo, Wanlun Ma, Lingyu Shen, Xi Xiao, Sheng Wen
First: 2026-07-29T16:07:19+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-29T16:07:19+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Fine-tuning is the dominant paradigm for specializing large language models (LLMs), yet it exposes a critical vulnerability: malicious data providers can embed harmful behaviors into downstream corpora, creating models that retain professional skills while violating human values on demand. Existing safety-realignment defenses often fail in practice due to three key limitations: they frequently cause catastrophic forgetting of specialized skills; their effectiveness collapses when the defender cannot observe the attacker's prompt template; and successfully realigned models remain susceptible to re-jailbreaking via simple system prompt switches. To address these challenges, we propose Routing-based On-Policy Distillation (ROPD), a novel realignment framework that models the divergence between aligned and compromised output probability distributions rather than fitting specific prompt templates. We conduct extensive experiments comparing ROPD against four state-of-the-art baselines across three datasets and three base models with varying alignment strengths. Our results demonstrate that when baseline defenses face template mismatches, often accompanied by severe degradation in downstream task performance. In contrast, ROPD substantially mitigates template-mismatch risks, maintaining superior robustness in both defense effectiveness and capability preservation. While our analysis indicates ROPD is not entirely immune to template shifts, its performance degradation is negligible compared to existing methods, establishing a new standard for robust LLM realignment.

微调是专门化大型语言模型（LLMs）的主流范式，但它暴露了一个关键漏洞：恶意数据提供者可以将有害行为嵌入下游语料库，创建在需求下保留专业技能但违反人类价值观的模型。现有的安全重对齐防御在实践中常常失败，主要有三个关键限制：它们经常导致专业技能的灾难性遗忘；当防御者无法观察攻击者的提示模板时，其有效性崩溃；成功重对齐的模型仍然容易通过简单的系统提示切换重新越狱。为了解决这些挑战，我们提出了基于路由的策略蒸馏（ROPD），这是一种新颖的重对齐框架，它建模对齐和妥协输出概率分布之间的差异，而不是拟合特定的提示模板。我们进行了广泛的实验，将ROPD与四个最先进的基线在三个数据集和三个基础模型上进行比较，后者具有不同的对齐强度。我们的结果表明，当基线防御面临模板不匹配时，通常伴随着下游任务性能的严重下降。相比之下，ROPD大大减轻了模板不匹配的风险，在防御有效性和能力保留方面保持了优越的鲁棒性。尽管我们的分析表明ROPD并非完全免疫于模板变化，但其性能下降与现有方法相比微不足道，为鲁棒的LLM重对齐建立了新的标准。

MEDIC: Comprehensive Evaluation of Leading Indicators for LLM Safety and Utility in Clinical Applications
Authors: Praveenkumar Kanithi, Clément Christophe, Marco AF Pimentel, Tathagata Raha, Prateek Munjal, Nada Saadi, Hamza A Javed, Svetlana Maslenkova, Nasir Hayat, Ronnie Rajan, Shadab Khan
First: 2024-09-11T14:44:51+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-28T17:58:21+00:00
Comments: Published in Transactions on Machine Learning Research (06/2026)
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
While Large Language Models (LLMs) achieve superhuman performance on standardized medical licensing exams, these static benchmarks have become saturated and increasingly disconnected from the functional requirements of clinical workflows. To bridge the gap between theoretical capability and verified utility, we introduce MEDIC, a comprehensive evaluation framework establishing leading indicators of clinical LLM competence across five dimensions. These upfront indicators reveal cross-benchmark capability gaps, such as the divergence between static knowledge retrieval and functional execution, that inform model selection before costly deployment-based evaluation. Beyond standard question-answering, we assess operational capabilities using deterministic execution protocols and a novel Cross-Examination Framework (CEF), which quantifies information fidelity and hallucination rates without reliance on reference texts. Our evaluation across a heterogeneous task suite exposes critical performance trade-offs: we identify a significant knowledge-execution gap, where proficiency in static retrieval does not predict success in operational tasks such as clinical calculation or SQL generation. Furthermore, we observe a divergence between passive safety (refusal) and active safety (error detection), revealing that models fine-tuned for high refusal rates often fail to reliably audit clinical documentation for factual accuracy. These findings demonstrate that no single architecture dominates across all dimensions, highlighting the necessity of a portfolio approach to clinical model deployment. We accompany this work with a publicly available MEDIC leaderboard at https://hf.co/spaces/m42-health/MEDIC-Benchmark.

GPT-Red: Automated Red Teaming via Self-Play at Scale
Authors: Eric Wallace, Christopher A. Choquette-Choo, Nikhil Kandpal, Sam Toyer, Dylan Hunn, Stephanie Lin, Yuxin Wen, Xiangyu Qi, Christopher Wolff, Zizhao Wang, Milad Nasr, Sicheng Zhu, Chuan Guo, Juan Felipe Cerón Uribe, Kaiwen Wang, Aiden Low, Kai Xiao, Kai Chen
First: 2026-07-28T16:03:39+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-28T16:03:39+00:00
Comments: 28 pages.13 main pages and 13 main figures
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
We introduce \textbf{GPT-Red}, an automated red-teaming agent that is trained to discover novel prompt injection attacks against frontier LLMs. The goal of this model is to evaluate and improve the robustness of our production systems. To this end, we use it to adversarially train GPT-5.6, our most robust model to prompt injections to date. To create GPT-Red, we design a scalable self-play algorithm where the model is tasked with attacking a diverse population of simultaneously-trained defender agents. We train the model on realistic red-teaming environments using compute on the same scale as some of our largest RL post-training runs, making it the single-largest LLM safety training run ever documented. GPT-Red excels at red-teaming: it reliably breaks our past models up to GPT-5.5, it finds more successful attacks than human red-teamers, and it generalizes to held-out environments, defender models, and harnesses. In the future, we expect that as we improve the robustness of each new GPT model, it will in turn will provide better learning signal for \textit{even stronger} red-teamer agents, thus unlocking a self-improvement flywheel.
中文标题/摘要

TRIDENT: Benchmarking LLM Safety in Finance, Medicine, and Law
Authors: Zheng Hui, Yijiang River Dong, Ehsan Shareghi, Nigel Collier
First: 2025-07-22T17:52:29+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-27T09:44:33+00:00
Comments: COLM 2026
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
As large language models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed in high-risk domains such as law, finance, and medicine, systematically evaluating their domain-specific safety and compliance becomes critical. While prior work has largely focused on improving LLM performance in these domains, it has often neglected the evaluation of domain-specific safety risks. To bridge this gap, we first define domain-specific safety principles for LLMs based on the AMA Principles of Medical Ethics, the ABA Model Rules of Professional Conduct, and the CFA Institute Code of Ethics. Building on this foundation, we introduce Trident-Bench, a benchmark specifically targeting LLM safety in the legal, financial, and medical domains. We evaluated 19 general-purpose and domain-specialized models on Trident-Bench and show that it effectively reveals key safety gaps -- strong generalist models (e.g., GPT, Gemini) can meet basic expectations, whereas domain-specialized models often struggle with subtle ethical nuances. This highlights an urgent need for finer-grained domain-specific safety improvements. By introducing Trident-Bench, our work provides one of the first systematic resources for studying LLM safety in law and finance, and lays the groundwork for future research aimed at reducing the safety risks of deploying LLMs in professionally regulated fields. Code and benchmark will be released at: https://github.com/zackhuiiiii/TRIDENT.
中文标题/摘要
标题：TRIDENT：金融、医学和法律领域大型语言模型安全性基准测试
随着大型语言模型（LLMs）在法律、金融和医学等高风险领域的日益应用，系统评估其领域特定的安全性和合规性变得至关重要。尽管之前的研究主要集中在提高LLM在这些领域的性能，但往往忽视了对领域特定安全风险的评估。为填补这一空白，我们首先基于医学伦理AMA原则、ABA职业行为模型规则和CFA协会伦理规范定义了LLM的领域特定安全原则。在此基础上，我们推出了Trident-Bench，这是一个专门针对法律、金融和医学领域LLM安全性的基准测试。我们在Trident-Bench上评估了19个通用和领域专用模型，结果表明它有效揭示了关键的安全差距——强大的通用模型（如GPT、Gemini）能够满足基本期望，而领域专用模型往往在微妙的伦理细节上表现不佳。这突显了对更细致的领域特定安全改进的迫切需求。通过引入Trident-Bench，我们的工作提供了研究法律和金融领域LLM安全性的首个系统资源之一，并为未来旨在降低在专业监管领域部署LLM的安全风险的研究奠定了基础。代码和基准测试将发布在：https://github.com/zackhuiiiii/TRIDENT。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical need for evaluating the safety and compliance of large language models (LLMs) in high-risk domains such as law, finance, and medicine, where prior studies have primarily focused on performance improvements while overlooking safety risks. Existing methods lack a systematic approach to assess domain-specific safety, leading to potential ethical issues. The proposed approach, Trident-Bench, establishes domain-specific safety principles derived from established ethical guidelines and benchmarks LLMs against these standards. This paper contributes by providing a structured resource for evaluating LLM safety in regulated fields, revealing that while generalist models meet basic safety expectations, specialized models often fail to address nuanced ethical concerns. The methodology involves evaluating 19 models on Trident-Bench, demonstrating significant safety gaps and underscoring the necessity for targeted improvements in LLM safety within these domains.
